Even in the case of `with_mean=False`, we update the mean anyway\n        # This is needed for the incremental computation of the var\n        # See incr_mean_variance_axis and _incremental_mean_variance_axis\n\n        # See docstring for motivation and alternatives.\"\n                )\n            sparse_constructor = (\n                sparse.csr_matrix if X.format == \"csr\" else sparse.csc_matrix\n            )\n\n            if self.with_std:\n                # First pass\n                if not hasattr(self, \"scale_\"):\n                    self.mean_, self.var_, self.n_samples_seen_ = mean_variance_axis(\n                        X, axis=0, weights=sample_weight, return_sum_weights=True\n                    )\n                # Next passes\n                else:\n                    (\n                        self.mean_,\n                        self.var_,\n                        self.n_samples_seen_,\n                    ) = incr_mean_variance_axis(\n                        X,\n                        axis=0,\n                        last_mean=self.mean_,\n                        last_var=self.var_,\n                        last_n=self.n_samples_seen_,\n                        weights=sample_weight,\n                    )\n                # We force the mean and variance to float64 for large arrays\n                # See https://github.com/scikit-learn/scikit-learn/pull/12338\n                self.mean_ = self.mean_.astype(np.float64, copy=False)\n                self.var_ = self.var_.astype(np.float64, copy=False)\n            else:\n                self.mean_ = None  # as with_mean must be False for sparse\n                self.var_ = None\n                weights = _check_sample_weight(sample_weight, X)\n                sum_weights_nan = weights @ sparse_constructor(\n                    (np.isnan(X.data), X.indices, X.indptr), shape=X.shape\n                )\n                self.n_samples_seen_ += (np.sum(weights) - sum_weights_nan).astype(","numLines":150,"startLine":880,"totalLines":3758}}}

This object dtype then causes issues in both scikit-learn validation and NumPy numerical operations.\n\n### Exact Location of Root Cause\n\n**File:** `/ccb_crossrepo/src/pandas/pandas/core/arrays/_utils.py`\n**Lines:** 45-48\n\n```python\nif na_value is lib.no_default:\n    if not is_nan_na():\n        na_value = NA\n        dtype = np.dtype(object)  # <-- ROOT CAUSE: Converts to object dtype\n```\n\nWhen `is_nan_na()` returns `False` (the default pandas configuration where `pd.NA` and `np.nan` are distinguished), nullable integer arrays with missing values are converted to **object dtype** arrays containing `pd.NA` values instead of `float64` arrays with `np.nan`.\n\n## Data Flow Trace\n\n### 1. scikit-learn Preprocessing Entry Point\n\n**File:** `/ccb_crossrepo/src/scikit-learn/sklearn/preprocessing/_data.py`\n\n- `StandardScaler.fit()` (line 899) calls `partial_fit()` (line 927)\n- `MinMaxScaler.fit()` (line 452) calls `partial_fit()` (line 474)\n- Both `partial_fit` methods call `validate_data()`:\n  - NumPy dtype Handling Issues\n\n**File:** `/ccb_crossrepo/src/numpy/numpy/_core/_methods.py`\n\nWhen NumPy receives an object dtype array, numerical operations like `_mean()` (line 115) and `_var()` (line 148) don't apply the automatic integer-to-float64 conversion:\n\n```python\n# Lines 125-127 in _mean():\nif dtype is None:\n    if issubclass(arr.dtype.type, (nt.integer, nt.bool)):\n        dtype = mu.dtype('f8')  # <-- This check FAILS for object dtype\n```\n\nObject dtype arrays bypass this conversion, leading to:\n1. Operations proceeding with object dtype\n2. Potential type errors when mathematical operations encounter `pd.NA`\n3. Unexpected results when `pd.NA` is treated as a regular Python object\n\n### 5. scikit-learn NA Detection Failure\n\n**File:** `/ccb_crossrepo/src/scikit-learn/sklearn/utils/validation.py`\n\nAt lines 116-118:\n```python\nif not is_array_api and X.dtype == np.dtype(\"object\") and not allow_nan:\n    if _object_dtype_isnan(X).any():\n        raise ValueError(\"Input contains NaN\")\n```\n\n**File:** `/ccb_crossrepo/src/scikit-learn/sklearn/utils/fixes.py`\n\nAt lines 53-54:\n```python\ndef _object_dtype_isnan(X):\n    return X != X  # Only detects np.nan, NOT pd.NA!\n```\n\nThis check uses `X != X`, which detects `np.nan` (since `np.nan != np.nan` is `True`) but **does NOT detect `pd.NA`** (since `pd.NA != pd.NA` returns `pd.NA`, not `True`).\n\n## Impact\n\n1. **Silent data corruption**: Nullable integer DataFrames may be converted to object dtype, potentially causing numerical operations to fail or produce incorrect results\n2. **Missing value detection bypass**: `pd.NA` values pass through scikit-learn's finite value checks undetected\n3. **Type errors**: NumPy ufuncs may fail when encountering `pd.NA` in object arrays\n4. **Inconsistent behavior**: Results depend on pandas configuration (`is_nan_na()` setting)\n\n## Recommended Fixes\n\n### Option 1: Fix in pandas (preferred)\nIn `/ccb_crossrepo/src/pandas/pandas/core/arrays/_utils.py` lines 45-50, always convert to float64 with np.nan for numeric array conversions requested by external libraries:\n\n```python\nif na_value is lib.no_default:\n    if not is_nan_na():\n        na_value = np.nan  # Use np.nan instead of pd.NA\n        # Don't override dtype to object\n```\n\n### Option 2: Fix in scikit-learn\nIn `/ccb_crossrepo/src/scikit-learn/sklearn/utils/validation.py`:\n1.
